acts by translation on
(G/B)^{\sigma}
, and it suffices to show that each
H
-orbit in
(G/B)^{\sigma}
is open, since then the orbits are finite in number and pairwise disjoint, hence open-closed. Indeed, for any geometric point
g
of
G
representing a point in
(G/B)^{\Sigma}
, the orbit map
G^{\sigma}\rightarrow(G/B)^{\sigma}
through
gB
is a smooth morphism by
[
CGP15
, Proposition A.8.10(2)]
again, hence has open image. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.2.2
.

Let
S
be a seaparated locally spatial diamond over
(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}
. Suppose that
G
has a
\Sigma
-stable Borel subgroup
B
. Let
B_{H}:=B^{\sigma}
, a Borel subgroup of
H
by Lemma
4.2.1
. Then the natural map

\Gr_{B_{H},S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}\rightarrow\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{B,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The map
\Gr_{B,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}\rightarrow\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{B,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}})
is a closed embedding by the same argument as for
[
SW20
, Lemma 19.1.5]
, hence qcqs, so by
[
Sch22
, Lemma 11.11]
it suffices to check that it induces a bijection on geometric points. A variation on
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
shows that the maps
\Gr_{B_{H},S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}\rightarrow\Gr_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}
and
\Gr_{B,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}
are bijections on geometric points. Hence in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{B_{H},S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{B,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}})}

both vertical maps and the bottom horizontal map induce bijections on geometric points (the latter by Proposition
4.1.2
). Therefore the upper horizontal map also induces a bijection on geometric points, concluding the proof.

∎

4.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.1.1

We write
LG^{\alg}
for the loop group of
G
with respect to the loop variable
t
, so
LG^{\alg}(R)=G(R((t)))
. We write
L^{+}G^{\alg}\subset LG^{\alg}
for the arc group, with functor of points
L^{+}G^{\alg}(R)=G(R[[t]])
. The algebraic affine Grassmannian is the fppf quotient
LG^{\alg}/L^{+}G^{\alg}
, which has a natural ind-scheme structure.

For the purpose of proving Proposition
4.1.1
, we may base change
G
to the algebraically closed field
\mathbf{C}
. Since
\sigma
is a semisimple automorphism of
G
over an algebraically closed field, a theorem of Steinberg
[
Ste68
, Theorem 7.5]
implies that
\sigma
stabilizes a Borus
(B,T)
of
G
. By Lemma
4.2.1
,
(B^{\sigma},T^{\sigma})
is a Borus of
H
.

4.3.1.
Iwahori stratification

Let
\Iw^{G}
be the Iwahori subgroup of
L^{+}G
corresponding to
B
. This induces a stratification by
\Iw^{G}
-orbits (cf.
[
RW22
, (4.6)]
)

\Gr_{G,\mathbf{C}}^{\alg,\red}=\coprod_{\lambda\in X_{*}(T)}\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg}.

Furthermore,
\Iw^{G}
is stable under
\sigma
and
\Iw^{H}:=(\Iw^{G})^{\sigma}
is the Iwahori subgroup of
L^{+}H
corresponding to
B^{\sigma}
, so we have an analogous stratification

\Gr_{H,\mathbf{C}}^{\alg,\red}=\coprod_{\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\sigma})}\Gr_{H,\lambda}^{\alg}.

The action of
\sigma
on
G
(stabilizing
T
) induces an action on
X_{*}(T)
. We will show that:

(1)

If
\lambda
is not fixed by
\sigma
, then
\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg}\cap(\sigma\cdot\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg})=\emptyset
.

(2)

If
\lambda
is fixed by
\sigma
, then
\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg})\xleftarrow{\sim}\Gr_{H,\lambda}^{\alg}
.

The combination of (1) and (2) clearly suffices to prove Proposition
4.1.1
.

Item (1) is immediate: since we arranged
\sigma
to preserve
\Iw^{G}
, we have
\sigma\cdot\Gr_{G,\lambda}=\Gr_{G,\sigma(\lambda)}
. Because distinct
\Iw^{G}
-orbits are disjoint, the intersection of any two distinct
\Iw^{G}
-orbits is empty.

Next we turn to (2). For this we analyze the structure of the strata.

4.3.2.
Fixed points of strata

For any root
\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}
, let
U_{\alpha}\subset G
be the corresponding root subgroup. For an affine root
\alpha+m\hbar
of
G
, denote by
U_{\alpha+m\hbar}
the corresponding affine root subgroup of
LG
(cf.
[
RW22
, §4.3]
), which for any isomorphism
u_{\alpha}\colon\mathbf{G}_{a}\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{\alpha}
, identifies with the image of
x\mapsto u_{\alpha}(xt^{m})
. Set

\delta_{\alpha}:=\begin{cases}1&\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}^{+},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and for
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)
define

(4.1)

\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}:=\prod_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}}\left(\prod_{\delta_{\alpha}\leq m<\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle}U_{\alpha+m\hbar}\right),

where the product may be taken in any order. Then as explained in the proof of
[
RW22
, Lemma 4.4]
, the action of
\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}
on
t^{\lambda}\in\Gr_{G}(\mathbf{C})
induces an isomorphism

(4.2)

\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg}.

Suppose
\sigma
fixes
\lambda
. Then (
4.2
) implies that

\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,\lambda}^{\alg})=(\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda})^{\sigma}\cdot t^{\lambda}.

Denote by
\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}(\alpha)
the factor of
\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}
in (
4.1
) indexed by
\alpha
. If
\sigma(\alpha)\neq\alpha
, then
\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}(\alpha)^{\sigma}=0
. On the other hand, if
\sigma\alpha=\alpha
then (using that
\sigma
was arranged to preserve
\mathfrak{R}^{+}
) we have
\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda}(\alpha)^{\sigma}=\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{H,\lambda}(\alpha)
, so that

(\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{G,\lambda})^{\sigma}\cdot t^{\lambda}\cong\prod_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}^{\sigma}}\mathrm{Iw}_{\mathrm{u}}^{H,\lambda}(\alpha)\cong\Gr_{H,\lambda}^{\alg}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
4.1.1
. ∎

4.4.
Moduli of local shtukas

We now turn to examine the moduli spaces of local shtukas, for which a reference is Scholze’s Berkeley lectures, especially
[
SW20
, Lecture XXIII]
.

Let
(G,b,\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I})
be a local shtuka datum (cf.
[
SW20
, Definition 23.1.1]
, except we allow the equal characteristic case as well):
G
is a reductive group over
E
,
b\in B(G)
, and
\mu_{i}
is a conjugacy class of cocharacters
\mathbf{G}_{m}\rightarrow G_{E^{s}}
. For any compact open subgroup
K\subset G(E)
there is a
moduli space of local shtukas with
K
-level structure

\Sht_{(G,b,\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}),K}
, which is functorial in
K
. There is a moduli description of
\Sht_{(G,b,\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}),K}
given (in the mixed characteristic case) in
[
SW20
, §23]
.

For
\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}\leq\{\mu_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
in the component-wise Bruhat order, there are closed embeddings
\Sht_{(G,b,\{\mu_{i}\}),K}\hookrightarrow\Sht_{(G,b,\{\mu_{i}^{\prime}\}),K}
. It will also be convenient to consider the following variant without any cocharacter “bounds”:

Definition 4.4.1
.

We define
\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}:=\varinjlim_{\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\Sht_{(G,b,\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}),K}
.

4.4.1.
Grothendieck-Messing period map

We recall some facts about the generalized Grothendieck-Messing period map

(4.3)

\pi_{\GM}^{G}\colon\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}\rightarrow\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}

from
[
SW20
, §23.5]
. Note that we have not imposed any “bound” above; our (
4.3
) comes from the colimit over
\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of the map from
[
SW20
, Corollary 23.5.3]
.

The image of
\pi_{\GM}^{G}
is an open subset of
\Gr^{\twi,a}_{G,I}\subset\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}
called the
admissible
locus. In terms of points,
\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}
parametrizes
G
-torsors
\mathcal{P}_{\eta}
on
\mathcal{Y}_{(0,\infty)}
plus additional data
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}
and
\iota_{r}
from
[
SW20
, Definition 23.5.1]
that we do not need to reference right now. The admissible locus is cut out by the condition that the Newton point
\nu_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}
and the Kottwitz point
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}
are both identically zero
[
SW20
, Theorem 22.6.2]
.

Definition 4.4.2
.

For a basic
b\in B(G)
, denote by
\Gr^{\twi,b}_{G,I}
the subspace where
\nu_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}=\nu(b)
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}=\kappa(b)
. This is an open subspace, and when
b=1_{G}
this recovers the admissible locus, i.e.,
\Gr^{\twi,1_{G}}_{G,I}=\Gr^{\twi,a}_{G,I}\subset\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}
.

Example 4.4.3
.

If
b\in B(G)
is basic, then there is a pure inner twist
G_{b}
(sometimes denoted
J_{b}
in the literature) and a canonical isomorphism

(4.4)

\operatorname{tr}_{b}\colon B(G)\xrightarrow{\sim}B(G_{b})

sending
b\in B(G)
to
1_{G_{b}}\in B(G_{b})
. There is also a compatible isomorphism
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi}\cong\Gr_{G_{b},I}^{\twi}
as in
[
FS21
, §III.4.1]
, which takes
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,b}
to
\Gr_{G_{b},I}^{\twi,a}
. So we could have formulated the subspaces
\Gr^{\twi,b}_{G,I}
as admissible loci for pure inner twists
G_{b}
. However, we prefer to keep the distinction, for psychological reasons if nothing else.

Let
\iota\colon B(H)\rightarrow B(G)
be the map induced by the inclusion
\iota\colon H\hookrightarrow G
. The induced map
\iota\colon\Gr_{H,I}^{\twi}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi}
is also a closed embedding (by the proof of
[
SW20
, Lemma 19.1.5]
), etc.

Remark 4.4.4
.

Since
X_{*}(T_{E^{s}}^{\sigma})_{\mathbf{Q}}\subset X_{*}(T_{E^{s}})_{\mathbf{Q}}
, the subset
\iota^{-1}(1_{G})\subset B(H)
consists of basic elements. For each
b^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(1_{G})
, there is an embedding

\iota_{b^{\prime}}\colon H_{b^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow G_{\iota(b^{\prime})}=G.

As a variant of
[
FS21
, Proposition III.4.2]
, this induces an embedding
\Gr_{H_{b^{\prime}},I}^{\twi}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi}
, identifying
\Gr_{H_{b^{\prime}},I}^{\twi,a}
with
\Gr_{H,I}^{\twi,b^{\prime}}
as sub-v-sheaves of
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi}
.

Lemma 4.4.5
.

We have

\Gr^{\twi,a}_{G,I}\cap\Gr^{\twi}_{H,I}=\coprod_{b^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(1_{G})}\Gr^{\twi,b^{\prime}}_{H,I}.

as subdiamonds of
\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}
.

Proof.

In Corollary
4.1.4
we have seen that
\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi})=\Gr_{H,I}^{\twi}
(with the obvious embedding). The admissible locus
\Gr^{\twi,a}_{G,I}\subset\Gr^{\twi}_{G,I}
is cut out by the condition that
\nu(\mathcal{P}_{\eta})=0
and
\kappa(\mathcal{P}_{\eta})=0
. Hence
\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,a})
is the pullback of the simultaneous vanishing loci of the Newton and Kottwitz maps from
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi}
to
\Gr_{H,I}^{\twi}
, which is precisely the expression on the RHS.
∎

4.4.2.
Fixed points of moduli of local shtukas

We now recall more about the structure of
\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}
in terms of the Grothendieck-Messing period map. According to
[
SW20
, Corollary 23.4.2]
,
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,a}
carries a
\underline{G(E)}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}
, and
\pi_{\GM}^{G}\colon\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}\rightarrow\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,a}
is the étale cover parametrizing
K
-lattices
\mathbb{P}^{G}\subset\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}
.

For basic
b^{\prime}\in B(G)
, note that
\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,b^{\prime}}
carries a
\underline{G_{b}^{\prime}(E)}
-torsor that we denote
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G,b^{\prime}}
, for example using the pure inner twisting procedure from
[
FS21
, Proposition III.4.2]
to reduce to the case where
b^{\prime}=1_{G}
where it is
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}
.

Definition 4.4.6
.

Let
b^{\prime}\in B(G)
be basic and
G_{b^{\prime}}
the corresponding inner twist of
G
. For a compact open subgroup
K\subset G_{b^{\prime}}(E)
, we denote by
\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}^{b^{\prime}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G,I}^{\twi,b^{\prime}}
the étale cover parametrizing
K
-lattices in
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G,b^{\prime}}
. Note that
G_{b}(E)
acts on
\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}^{b^{\prime}}
by change of framing.

In fact, the pure inner twisting procedure of
[
FS21
, Proposition III.4.2]
gives a
G_{b}(E)
-equivariant isomorphism between
\Sht_{(G,b,I),K}^{b^{\prime}}
and
\Sht_{(G_{b^{\prime}},b^{\prime\prime},I),K}
for
b^{\prime\prime}\in B(G)
such that the inner twist of
G_{b^{\prime}}
corresponding to
b^{\prime\prime}
is isomorphic to
G_{b}
, so it was unnecessary to introduce this new notation. However, we like to use it to distinguish what arises naturally from calculations.

For
b^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(1_{G})\subset B(H)
, write
\iota^{*}_{b^{\prime}}K\subset H_{b^{\prime}}(E)
for the pre-image of
K
under the map
\iota_{b^{\prime}}\colon H_{b^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow G
from Remark
4.4.4
. We abbreviate
\iota^{*}K=\iota^{*}_{1_{H}}K=K^{\sigma}\subset H(E)
.

Proposition 4.4.7
.

Suppose
K\subset G(E)
is a
\Sigma
-stable open compact subgroup, with prime-to-
\ell
pro-order. Then we have

\Fix(\sigma,\Sht_{(G,1_{G},I),K})=\coprod_{b^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(1_{G})}\Sht_{(H,1_{H},I),\iota^{*}_{b^{\prime}}K}^{b^{\prime}}

as sub-v-sheaves of
\Sht_{(G,1_{G},I),K}
(with embedding on the RHS induced by
\iota_{b^{\prime}}
of Remark
4.4.4
). In particular,
\Sht_{(H,1_{H},I),\iota^{*}K}
is an open-closed subdiamond of
\Fix(\sigma,\Sht_{(G,1_{G},I),K})
.

Remark 4.4.8
.

As explained in Definition
4.4.6
, the term
\Sht_{(H,1_{H},I),\iota^{*}_{b^{\prime}}K}^{b^{\prime}}
is
H(E)
-equivariantly isomorphic to the local Shimura variety
\Sht_{(H_{b^{\prime}},b,I),\iota^{*}_{b^{\prime}}K}
where
b\in B(H_{b^{\prime}})
is such that the inner twist
(H_{b^{\prime}})_{b}
is isomorphic to
H
. Only the assertion that
\Sht_{(H,1_{H},I),\iota^{*}K}
is an open-closed subdiamond of
\Fix(\sigma,\Sht_{(G,1_{G},I),K})
, will be really crucial in future sections.

Proof.

We have already calculated the
\sigma
-fixed points in the image of the Grothendieck-Messing period map
\pi_{\GM}^{G}
in Lemma
4.4.5
, and in view of the answer, it suffices to show that for all
x\in\Gr^{\twi,b^{\prime}}_{H,I}
, the inclusion
\Sht_{(H,1_{H},I),\iota^{*}_{b^{\prime}}K}^{b^{\prime}}\rightarrow\Sht_{(G,1_{G},I),K}
sends
(\pi_{\GM}^{H})^{-1}(x)
isomorphically to
\Fix(\sigma,(\pi_{\GM}^{G})^{-1}(x))
. By pure inner twisting procedure, as explained in Remark
4.4.4
and Remark
4.4.8
, we may reduce this statement to the case where
b^{\prime}=1
. The
\underline{G(E)}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}
corresponding to
x
has an
H
-structure
\mathbb{P}^{H}_{\eta}
since
x
lies in the image of
\pi_{\GM}^{H}
, and
(\pi_{\GM}^{H})^{-1}(x)
parametrizes
\iota^{*}K
-lattices in
\mathbb{P}^{H}_{\eta}
. The action of
H(E)
on a fixed
\iota^{*}K
-lattice
L_{0}
induces an isomorphism
(\pi_{\GM}^{H})^{-1}(x)\cong H(E)/\iota^{*}K
.

On the other hand,
\Fix(\sigma,(\pi_{\GM}^{G})^{-1}(x))
parametrizes
K
-lattices in
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}
which are stable under
\Sigma
. As
\mathbb{P}_{\eta}^{G}\cong\mathbb{P}^{H}_{\eta}\times^{H(E)}G(E)
, the action of
G(E)
on
L_{0}
induces an isomorphism
\Fix(\sigma,(\pi_{\GM}^{G})^{-1}(x))\cong\Fix(\sigma,G(E)/K)
, with
\sigma
acting in the natural way. Hence it suffices to see that

(4.5)

H(E)/K^{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\sim}(G(E)/K)^{\sigma}.

The long exact sequence of non-abelian cohomology of
\Sigma
reads

K^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow G(E)^{\sigma}\rightarrow(G(E)/K)^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;G(E)).

Since
K
has prime-to-
\ell
pro-order by assumption, we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)=0
. This shows (
4.5
), completing the proof.
∎

5.
Parity sheaf theory on
p
-adic affine Grassmannians

The theory of
parity sheaves
, originating in work of Juteau-Mautner-Williamson
[
JMW14
]
, has driven many recent developments in geometric representation theory: see
[
AR15
,
AR16
,
MR18
,
AMRW19
,
Wil18
]
for a sampling of applications. Parity sheaves are defined somewhat similarly to perverse sheaves, but with
parity
conditions instead of inequalities on cohomological degrees.

In this section, we develop a theory of
relative
parity sheaves on affine Grassmannians arising in
p
-adic geometry. The usage of the adjective “relative” is in the same sense as “relative perversity” of Hansen-Scholze
[
HS23
]
: it means parity along the geometric fibers of a morphism. In practice, the morphism is usually the projection of a Beilinson-Drinfeld type affine Grassmannian to the base. Leslie-Lonergan
[
LL21
]
introduced the “Tate-parity sheaves” as the analogue of parity sheaves in Tate categories, and we also develop a theory of “relative Tate-parity sheaves” on families of affine Grassmannians.

The main results of the structure theory of relative (Tate-)parity sheaves include the calculation of Ext groups, the vanishing of maps between incongruous objects, the fact that tilting objects are relative Tate-parity, and the generation of all relative (Tate-)parity sheaves by direct sums of shifts of tilting objects. Some of the difficulties are similar to those present in the theory of perverse sheaves on
p
-adic affine Grassmannians: the lack of a definitive dimension theory for adic spaces, and the failure of constructibility for the sheaves of interest. There are also some new technical issues, coming from the fact that the Ext-vanishing properties underlying the structure theory of parity sheaves have an “absolute” nature, which is in tension with the “relative” nature of our definitions. Moreover, the Krull-Schmidt property, which was an important technical property underpinning
[
RW22
]
and
[
LL21
]
, fails badly in the relative situation, due to the complicated nature of local systems on a general profinite set. The theory of ULA sheaves developed in
[
FS21
]
ultimately helps us to overcome these difficulties.

We now give an overview of the contents of this section. In §
5.1
we define relative parity complexes, etc. on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
for a small v-stack
S
over
\Div_{X}^{1}
and establish their structure theory (mostly for the case where
S
is strictly totally disconnected). In §
5.2
we introduce a “small” version of the Tate category for
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
, the adjective “small” referring to that the sheaves are required to have strong finiteness properties, and a “small” version of Smith-Treumann localization. In §
5.3
we define relative Tate-parity complexes, etc. on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
and establish their structure theory. In §
5.4
we define “even maps” and prove that they preserve relative parity, and next in §
5.5
we apply this to certain Demazure resolutions in order to show that relative parity sheaves exist for all strata, and that relative parity is preserved by convolution. In §
5.3.3
we study the interaction of relative parity sheaves with modular reduction
\mathbb{F}
and the operation
\mathbb{T}
. This is used in §
5.6
to define the “lifting functor” à la Leslie-Lonergan, which will constitute a key step in the construction of the Brauer functor.

5.1.
Relative parity sheaves

Let
S
be a small v-stack with a map
S\rightarrow\Div^{1}_{X}
. Then we may form
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}:=\Gr_{G,\Div^{1}_{X}}\times_{\Div_{X}^{1}}S
and
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}:=\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,\Div^{1}_{X}}\times_{\Div_{X}^{1}}S
as in
[
FS21
, §VI]
.

5.1.1.
Schubert stratification

Suppose
S\in\Perf
is strictly totally disconnected. Then
[
FS21
, VI.2]
applies and we have a presentation

\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}=\varinjlim_{\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}}\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}

where
T
is a split maximal torus in
G_{E^{s}}
; here
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
is the subfunctor of
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
parametrizing modifications which are given by some
\mu^{\prime}\leq\mu
at each geometric point of
S
. The open subfunctor
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
is defined as the complement of
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu^{\prime}}
for
\mu^{\prime}<\mu
.

The pullback of
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
) to
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
is denoted
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
(resp.
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
). Let
L^{+}_{S/\Div^{1}_{X}}G:=L^{+}_{\Div^{1}_{X}}G\times_{\Div_{X}^{1}}S
. Then
L^{+}_{S/\Div^{1}_{X}}G
acts on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
by left translation, and the orbits are the
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
.

We denote by
i_{\mu}
the locally closed embedding
i_{\mu}\colon\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
. By
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.2.4]
,
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
has the structure of a fibration over the diamond of the (opposite) partial flag variety
(P_{\mu}^{-})^{\diamond}_{S}
, with the fibers being iterated extensions of
(\Lie G)^{\diamond}_{\mu\leq m}\{m\}
where
(\Lie G)_{\mu\leq m}
is the subspace on which
\mathbf{G}_{m}
acts with weights
\leq m
via the adjoint action composed with
\mu
. (Here
\{m\}
is a “Breuil-Kisin twist”.)

Lemma 5.1.1
.

For the projection

\pi\colon\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}\rightarrow S
, each
\mathrm{R}^{n}\pi_{*}\Lambda
has
\Lambda
-free geometric stalks and vanishes if
n
is odd.

Proof.

Both
(P_{\mu}^{-})^{\diamond}
and
(\Lie G)^{\diamond}_{\mu\leq m}
have the property that their cohomology with constant coefficients is supported in even degrees. The preceding description shows that
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
is an iterated fibration over
(P_{\mu}^{-})^{\diamond}_{S}
with fibers of the form
(\Lie G)^{\diamond}_{\mu\leq m}
(note that the Breuil-Kisin twist is trivializable over geometric points), so the result follows from the Serre spectral sequence, which is forced to degenerate by the even-ness.
∎

5.1.2.
Categories of sheaves

We will take coefficients in a ring
\Lambda
which is an
\ell
-adically complete local PID over
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}
, i.e., a field of characteristic
\ell
or a complete DVR with residue characteristic
\ell
. In our applications of interest,
\Lambda
will be either
k
or
\mathbb{O}:=W(k)
.

Definition 5.1.2
.

We define
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
to be the full subcategory of
D_{\et}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
spanned by the image of
D_{\et}^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
under
*
-pullback.

Remark 5.1.3

(Stability under six operations)
.

As a consequence of
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.6.6]
, the category
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is stable under the operations of Verdier duality,
-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}_{\Lambda}-
,
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{H}om_{\Lambda}(-,-)
,
i_{!}i^{*},\mathrm{R}i_{*}i^{*},i_{!}i^{!},\mathrm{R}i_{*}i^{!}
where
i=i_{\mu}
is the inclusion of any stratum. (The analogous statement does
not
hold in the “multiple legs” situation, unless the map from
S
factors through the locus where the legs are disjoint.)

Recall that an additive category is called
Krull-Schmidt
if each object is a finite direct sum of indecomposable objects with local endomorphism rings (in particular, this implies that an object is indecomposable if and only if its endomorphism ring is local). We will show that
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
is Krull-Schmidt when
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
is a geometric point.

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Then all objects of
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
admit a finite filtration by objects of the form
i_{\mu!}\Lambda
, and also a finite filtration by objects of the form
\mathrm{R}i_{\mu*}\Lambda
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.5]
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Then for any object
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
,
\End(\mathcal{K})
is finitely generated as a
\Lambda
-module.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
5.1.4
, it suffices to show that for each
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
,

\Hom(i_{\mu!}\Lambda,\mathrm{R}i_{\mu^{\prime}*}\Lambda)\cong\Hom(i^{*}_{\mu^{\prime}}i_{\mu!}\Lambda,\Lambda)\text{
is finitely generated as a $\Lambda$-module.}

If
\mu\neq\mu^{\prime}
, then
i^{*}_{\mu^{\prime}}i_{\mu!}\Lambda=0
. If
\mu^{\prime}=\mu
, then
i^{*}_{\mu^{\prime}}i_{\mu!}\Lambda\cong\Lambda
, so that
\Hom(i^{*}_{\mu^{\prime}}i_{\mu!}\Lambda,\Lambda)\cong\Lambda
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Then the category
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is Krull-Schmidt.

Proof.

The usual t-structure on the category
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is bounded. Therefore, it is Karoubian (i.e., every idempotent splits) by
[
LC07
, Theorem]
. By
[
CYZ08
, Theorem A.1]
, a Karoubian category such that
\End(\mathcal{K})
is semiperfect (cf.
[
Lam01
, Chapter 8]
for a reference on semiperfect rings) for every
\mathcal{K}
is Krull-Schmidt. It therefore suffices to show that
\End(\mathcal{K})
is semiperfect for every
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
. For this we note that any finite
\Lambda
-algebra is semiperfect, and we showed in Lemma
5.1.5
that
\End(\mathcal{K})
is a finite
\Lambda
-module.
∎

5.1.3.
Relative parity complexes

A
pariversity
on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
is a function
\dagger
from
X_{*}(T)^{+}
, thought of as the set of strata on the geometric fibers over
S
, to
\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
.

Example 5.1.7

(Dimension pariversity)
.

In this section we fix
\dagger
to be the “dimension pariversity”
\dagger_{G}
, defined by

\dagger_{G}(\lambda):=\langle 2\rho,\lambda\rangle\pmod{2}\in\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}

where
2\rho
is the sum of the positive roots of
G
.

The definition below is a “relative to
S
” (in a sense parallel to the notion of relative perversity in
[
FS21
, §VI.7]
) version of the definition of parity sheaves in
[
JMW14
, Definition 2.4]
.

Definition 5.1.8

(Parity complexes)
.

Let
S
be a small v-stack. For a geometric point
\overline{s}=\Spa(C,C^{+})\rightarrow S
and
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
, we denote by
\mathcal{K}|_{\overline{s}}
the
*
-restriction of
\mathcal{K}
along
\Gr_{G,\overline{s}/\Div^{1}_{X}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
. For each
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
we let
i_{\mu}\colon\Gr_{G,\overline{s}/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,\overline{s}/\Div^{1}_{X}}
be the locally closed embedding of the corresponding stratum.

(1)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is
relative
?
-even
if for all geometric points
\overline{s}\rightarrow S
and all
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
and all
n\in\mathbf{Z}
, the cohomology sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n}(i_{\mu}^{?}(\mathcal{K}|_{\overline{s}}))
is constant and
\Lambda
-free, and vanishes for
n\not\equiv\dagger(\mu)\pmod{2}
.

(2)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say that
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is
relative
?
-odd
if
\mathcal{K}[1]
is relative
?
-even.

(3)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say that
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is
relative
?
-parity
if
\mathcal{K}
is either relative
?
-even or relative
?
-odd.

(4)

We say
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is a
relative even complex
if
\mathcal{K}
is both relative
*
-even and relative
!
-even. We say that
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is a
relative odd complex
if
\mathcal{K}[1]
is even.

(5)

We say
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is a
relative parity complex
if it is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of relative even and relative odd complexes. The full subcategory of
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
spanned by relative parity complexes is denoted
\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
.

The following result is part of
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.6.6]
, but we state it for emphasis and take the opportunity to spell out the proof.

Lemma 5.1.9

(
[
FS21
]
)
.

Let
f\colon S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a map of small v-stacks over
\Div_{X}^{1}
and
i_{\mu}\colon\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
be the locally closed immersion of a Schubert cell. Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,S^{\prime}/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,S^{\prime}/\Div^{1}_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where all squares are Cartesian. Then we have a natural isomorphism
f_{\mu}^{*}(i_{\mu})^{!}\cong(i_{\mu}^{\prime})^{!}\widetilde{f}^{*}
of functors
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\Lambda)^{\bd}\rightarrow D_{(L^{+}G)}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S^{\prime}/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu};\Lambda)^{\bd}
.

Proof.

We abbreviate
\mathbb{D}_{/S}
(resp.
\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}
) for the relative Verdier duality over
S
(resp.
S^{\prime}
). By
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.15]
, relative Verdier duality is compatible with base change, meaning there are natural isomorphisms

(5.1)

f_{\mu}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}f_{\mu}^{*}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{f}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}\widetilde{f}^{*}.

Using also that
i_{\mu}^{!}\cong\mathbb{D}_{/S}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}
and
(i_{\mu}^{\prime})^{!}\cong\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}(i^{\prime}_{\mu})^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}
on ULA objects (because relative Verdier duality is involutive on ULA objects
[
FS21
, Corollary IV.2.25]
), we have natural isomorphisms

f_{\mu}^{*}i_{\mu}^{!}\cong f_{\mu}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: ula base change 1}}}{{\cong}}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}f_{\mu}^{*}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}\cong\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}(i^{\prime}_{\mu})^{*}\widetilde{f}^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: ula base change 1}}}{{\cong}}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}(i^{\prime}_{\mu})^{*}\mathbb{D}_{/S^{\prime}}\widetilde{f}^{*}\cong(i_{\mu}^{\prime})^{!}\widetilde{f}^{*}.

∎

5.1.4.
Structure theory for strictly totally disconnected
S

Here we prove several structural results about
\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
, parallel to
[
JMW14
, §2]
,
under the assumption that
S
is strictly totally disconnected.
In particular, thanks this assumption, we have the stratification of
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
by
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1},\mu}
, ranging over
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
, and we let
i_{\mu}
be the locally closed embedding
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1},\mu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
.
Below, all Hom and Ext groups are taken in the category

D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
, which we omit for ease of notation.

Lemma
5.1.9
implies the following connection between relative parity as in Definition
5.1.8
, and an “absolute” notion of parity.

Corollary 5.1.10
.

Let
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
. For
?\in\{*,!\}
, if
\mathcal{K}
is relative ?-even then for all
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
and all
n\in\mathbf{Z}
, the cohomology sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{n}(i_{\mu}^{?}(\mathcal{K}))
is constant and
\Lambda
-free, and vanishes for
n\not\equiv\dagger(\mu)\pmod{2}
.

Proof.

The constancy is automatic from the definition of
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
. The
\Lambda
-freeness of
?
-restrictions can be checked after base changing to geometric points
\overline{s}\rightarrow S
, hence follows from the definition of relative
?
-even, using Lemma
5.1.9
to commute base change on
S
with
i_{\mu}^{!}
. The fact that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(i_{\mu}^{*}(\mathcal{K}))
vanishes for
n\not\equiv\dagger(\mu)\pmod{2}
follows immediately from the definition of relative ?-even, while the fact that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(i_{\mu}^{!}(\mathcal{K}))
vanishes for
n\not\equiv\dagger(\mu)\pmod{2}
can be checked after base change along geometric points
\overline{s}=\Spa(C,C^{+})\rightarrow S
, where it follows from the definition of relative ?-even after applying Lemma
5.1.9
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.11
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected. If
\mathcal{F}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is relative
*
-parity and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is relative
!
-parity, then we have a (non-canonical) isomorphism of
\Lambda
-modules

(5.2)

\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\bigoplus_{\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}}\Ext^{\bullet}(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{G})

and both sides are finite projective over
C^{\infty}(|S|,\Lambda)=\Ext^{\bullet}_{S}(\Lambda,\Lambda)
, the ring of continuous functions on
|S|
valued in
\Lambda
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to that for
[
JMW14
, Proposition 2.6]
, but we have to address some issues related to the discrepancy between our relative situation and the “absolute” situation of
[
JMW14
]
. Since the statement is compatible with finite direct sums and shifts, we may assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{F}
is relative
*
-even and
\mathcal{G}
is relative
!
-even.

We will show by induction, on the number
M
of
\mu
such that
\supp(\mathcal{F})
intersects nontrivially with
\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1},\mu}
, that

(i)

\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is finite projective over
C^{\infty}(|S|,\Lambda)
and vanishes in odd degrees, and

(ii)

satisfies the decomposition (
5.2
).

If
M=1
, then
\mathcal{F}\cong i_{\mu!}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
for some
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
, and we have

\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\Ext^{\bullet}(i_{\mu!}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\Ext^{\bullet}(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{G}).

This shows part (ii) of the inductive hypothesis. By Corollary
5.1.10
,
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
and
i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{G}
are both locally constant complexes on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
concentrated in
even
degrees. Then part (i) of the induction hypothesis follows from Lemma
5.1.1
(here we use the assumption that
S
is strictly totally disconnected, so it has no Exts between locally constant sheaves).

If
M>1
, let
i
be the inclusion of a closed stratum in the support of
\mathcal{F}
and consider the excision sequence
j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}
. Applying
\Hom(-,\mathcal{G})
, we get a long exact sequence

(5.3)

\ldots\rightarrow\Ext^{n}(i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Ext^{n}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Ext^{n}(j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\ldots

Rewriting
\Ext^{n}(i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\Ext^{n}(i^{*}\mathcal{F},i^{!}\mathcal{G})
and
\Ext^{n}(j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\Ext^{n}(j^{*}\mathcal{F},j^{!}\mathcal{G})
, the induction hypothesis applies to the flanking terms. In particular, (i) implies that the long exact sequence (
5.3
) splits, and then (i) and (ii) for the flanking terms imply (i) and (ii) for
\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
.
∎

Corollary 5.1.12
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected. If
\mathcal{F}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is relative
*
-even and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is relative
!
-odd, then
\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=0
.

Lemma 5.1.13
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected. Suppose
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
are either both relative even or both relative odd. If
i_{\mu}
is the inclusion of a stratum which is open in the support of both
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, then the restriction map
\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Hom(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G})
is surjective.

Proof.

Since the statement is compatible with simultaneous shifts on
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, it suffices to treat the case where
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
are both relative even. Let
i
be in the inclusion of the complementary strata, so that we have an excision triangle of relative
!
-even complexes,

i_{*}i^{!}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\rightarrow i_{\mu*}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G}.

Applying
\Hom(\mathcal{F},-)
, we obtain a long exact sequence

\ldots\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Hom(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{F},i_{*}i^{!}\mathcal{G}[1])\rightarrow\ldots

in which
\Hom(\mathcal{F},i_{*}i^{!}\mathcal{G}[1])=0
by Corollary
5.1.12
, because
\mathcal{G}[1]
is relative
!
-odd, so the first map is surjective.
∎

Lemma 5.1.14
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
be an indecomposable relative parity complex, and let
j\colon U\rightarrow\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
be an inclusion of a union of strata open in the support of
\mathcal{F}
. Then
j^{*}\mathcal{F}
is either zero or indecomposable.

Proof.

By the assumption on
S
, Lemma
5.1.6
applies to show that
D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})^{\bd}
is Krull-Schmidt. Hence the indecomposability of
\mathcal{F}
implies that
\End(\mathcal{F})
is local. As a quotient of a local ring is local, Lemma
5.1.13
implies that
\Hom(j^{*}\mathcal{F},j^{*}\mathcal{F})
is also local, so
j^{*}\mathcal{F}
is either zero or indecomposable.
∎

Proposition 5.1.15
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
be an indecomposable relative parity complex. Then
\mathcal{F}
enjoys the following properties:

(1)

\mathcal{F}
has support of the form
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
for some
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
.

(2)

The restriction
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
to the open stratum in
\supp(\mathcal{F})
is a shifted
\Lambda
-free constant sheaf
\Lambda[d]
.

(3)

Any indecomposable relative parity complex
\mathcal{G}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
supported on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
, such that
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G}\cong\Lambda[d]
on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
, is isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

(1) If
\supp(\mathcal{F})
contains two disjoint open strata
U,U^{\prime}
then applying Lemma
5.1.13
to each of
U
and
U^{\prime}
shows that
\End(\mathcal{F})
is not a local ring. But since
\mathcal{F}
is indecomposable, the Krull-Schmidt property (Lemma
5.1.6
) implies that
\End(\mathcal{F})
is local.

(2) Follows from Lemma
5.1.14
and [Proposition VI.6.5]FS, using assumption that
S
is a geometric point.

(3) Since
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be indecomposable, the Krull-Schmidt property (Lemma
5.1.6
) implies that
\End(\mathcal{F})
is a local ring. By assumption, there are isomorphisms
\alpha\colon i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G}
and
\beta\colon i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
which are mutual inverses. By Lemma
5.1.13
we can find lifts
\widetilde{\alpha}\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
and
\widetilde{\beta}\colon\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
such that
\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}\in\End(\mathcal{F})
does not lie in the unique maximal ideal of
\End(\mathcal{F})
, hence is invertible. Similarly,
\widetilde{\alpha}\circ\widetilde{\beta}\in\End(\mathcal{G})
is invertible. Therefore,
\widetilde{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{\beta}
are isomorphisms.

∎

Remark 5.1.16
.

Lemma
5.1.14
and Proposition
5.1.15
fail badly for general strictly totally disconnected
S
. Indeed, if
S
is infinite, then any non-zero locally constant sheaf on
S
can be decomposed non-trivially into a direct sum by decomposing its support into a finite union of closed-open subsets. Therefore, there are no non-trivial indecomposable objects. In particular, the results of §
5.1.2
are not true for general strictly totally disconnected
S
.
6
6

6

This corrects a mistake in a previous version of this paper, which was pointed out to us by David Hansen.
This represents a significant departure from how the theory works in
[
JMW14
]
.

In the next section, we will establish the following facts.

•

For a v-stack
S
, we let
\Loc(S;\Lambda)
be the category of
\Lambda
-free étale local systems on
S
. Then for any v-stack
S\rightarrow\Div_{X}^{1}
, there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence (
6.2
)

\Sat(\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}};\Lambda)\cong\Rep_{\Loc(S;\Lambda)}(\check{G}),

an incarnation of the Geometric Satake equivalence relative to
S
. (The Satake category
\Sat(\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}};\Lambda)\subset D_{\et}^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
is the full subcategory spanned by flat perverse sheaves over
S
.)

•

For
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
, let
T_{\Lambda}(\mu)\in\Rep_{\Lambda}(\check{G})
be the tilting module with highest weight
\mu
. Assume
\ell>b(\check{G})
. Then for any
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\Lambda)
and any
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
, the image
\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
of
\mathcal{L}\otimes T_{\Lambda}(\mu)\in\Rep_{\Loc(S;\Lambda)}(\check{G})
under (
6.2
) is relative parity (Corollary
6.2.4
).

We assume these facts for now.

Then we have the following generalization of Proposition
5.1.15
for strictly totally disconnected
S
.

Proposition 5.1.17
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected and
\ell>b(\check{G})
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
and
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
be maximal so that the support of
\mathcal{F}
intersects
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
non-trivially. If
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{L}[\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle]
on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
for some
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S,\Lambda)
, then
\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
is a retract of
\mathcal{F}
.

(2)

Any
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\Lambda})
is a finite direct sum of shifts of
\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
for various
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\Lambda)
.

Proof.

(1) It is immediate from the construction of
\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
that
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{L}[\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle]
. Hence the assumption implies that there are isomorphisms
\alpha\colon i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
and
\beta\colon i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})\rightarrow i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
which are mutual inverses. By Lemma
5.1.13
we can find lifts
\widetilde{\alpha}\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
and
\widetilde{\beta}\colon\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
restricting to
\alpha
and
\beta
under
i_{\mu}^{*}
. It then suffices to show that
\widetilde{\alpha}\circ\widetilde{\beta}\in\End(\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L}))
is an isomorphism. This can be checked after pulling back along all geometric points
\Spa(C,C^{+})\rightarrow S
, and then it follows from Proposition
5.1.15
(3).

(2) We prove the statement by induction on the largest
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
intersecting
\supp(\mathcal{F})
non-trivially. We have
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}[-\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle]=\bigoplus\mathcal{L}_{j}[d_{j}]
for some
\mathcal{L}_{j}\in\Loc(S;\Lambda)
and
d_{j}\in\mathbf{Z}
by
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.5]
and the assumption that
S
is strictly totally disconnected. Then part (1) implies that
\mathcal{F}\cong\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L}_{j})[d_{j}]\oplus\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
where the inductive hypothesis applies to
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
∎

5.2.
Small Smith-Treumann localization

We develop here a version of the Tate category and Smith-Treumann localization which is “small” in the sense that it consists only of sheaves satisfying strong finiteness conditions. It is more similar to the formalism of
[
Tre19
,
RW22
]
, but we note that our sheaves are still not “constructible” since the Schubert stratifications are not constructible in
p
-adic geometry (due to failure of quasicompactness). The structure theory of ULA sheaves (relative to
S
) on
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
from
[
FS21
]
is what makes this small version well behaved even in the absence of quasicompactness.

5.2.1.
The small Tate category

We continue to assume that
\Lambda
is an
\ell
-adically complete PID over
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}
. Let
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}\subset D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
be the full subcategory spanned by objects with finite tor-dimension over
\Lambda[\Sigma]
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

We define the
small Tate category
of
\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
to be the Verdier quotient

\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}:=\frac{D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}}{\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}}

and the
small Tate category
of
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
to be

\Perf^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}:=\frac{D_{\et}^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}}{\Perf^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}}.

We make analogous definitions for
\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I},\mu}
and
\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I},\leq\mu}
, etc.

Parallel to Lemma
3.2.4
, we give an alternate characterization of
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}
; note that this one applies without assuming that
\Lambda=k
is a field.

Lemma 5.2.2
.

The subcategory
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}\subset D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}
coincides with the full subcategory spanned by objects whose stalks at all geometric points are perfect complexes over
\Lambda[\Sigma]
.

Proof.

All geometric stalks of all objects of
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}
are pseudocoherent by
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.5]
, and have finite tor-amplitude by
[
Sta20
, Tag 0DJJ]
. Pseudo-coherent plus finite tor-amplitude implies perfect by
[
Sta20
, Tag 0656]
; this shows one containment.

For the other containment, let
\mathcal{K}\in D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}
; we must show that if all geometric stalks of
\mathcal{K}
are perfect complexes over
\Lambda[\Sigma]
, then
\mathcal{K}
has finite tor-amplitude. By
[
Sta20
, Tag 0DJJ]
, it suffices to exhibit a uniform bound on the tor-amplitude of the stalks. The rest of the proof is as for Lemma
3.2.4
, except we use that for a perfect complex over
\Lambda[\Sigma]
, the properties of being flat and projective coincide by
[
Sta20
, Tag 051E]
.
∎

Remark 5.2.3

(Comparison to the large Tate category)
.

The tautological fully faithful embedding

D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}\hookrightarrow D^{b}_{\et}(\Gr_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])

carries
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}
into
\Flat^{b}(\Gr_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])
and so induces a functor

(5.4)

\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}\rightarrow\Shv(\Gr_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})

which is conservative; similarly for the equivariant version.

Definition 5.2.4
.

We denote by

\mathbb{T}^{*}\colon D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}

the tautological projection.

We denote by

\epsilon^{*}\colon D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}\rightarrow D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda[\Sigma])^{\bd}

the inflation via the augmentation
\Lambda[\Sigma]\rightarrow\Lambda
.

We write

\mathbb{T}:=\mathbb{T}^{*}\varepsilon^{*}\colon D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}.

These functors are compatible with the ones having the same notations in §
3.2
, under (
5.4
). We use the same notation for the analogous operations for strata, Schubert varieties, and
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{H,S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}
, etc.

5.2.2.
The small
\Psm
operation

We now define the version of the “Smith operation”
\Psm
from §
3.6
for small Tate categories. We assume the setup of §
4
:
G
has a
\Sigma
-action and
H:=H^{\sigma}
is reductive, so that
\Fix(\sigma,\Gr_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}})=\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
by Proposition
4.1.2
.

Lemma 5.2.5
.

Let
\iota\colon\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
be the inclusion of the
\Sigma
-fixed points. For any
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\ULA}_{\et}(\Gr_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
, the restriction
\iota^{*}\mathcal{F}\in D^{\ULA}_{\et}(\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
is ULA over
S
.

Proof.

The condition can be checked v-locally on
S
by
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.5]
. Therefore, we may assume that
G
has a Borus
(B,T)
. Let
(B_{H},T_{H})=(B^{\sigma},T^{\sigma})
be the corresponding Borus of
H
(cf. Lemma
4.2.1
). Recall the constant term functors
\CT
from
[
FS21
, §VI.3]
. Applying (a variant for
(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}
of)
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.4]
, it suffices to check that
\CT_{B_{H}}(\iota^{*}\mathcal{F})
is ULA over
S
. By proper base change applied to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{T,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{B,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{T_{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{B_{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{H,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

all of whose squares are Cartesian (thanks to Proposition
4.1.2
and Proposition
4.2.2
),
we have a natural isomorphism
\CT_{B_{H}}(\iota^{*}\mathcal{F})\cong\iota^{*}\CT_{B}(\mathcal{F})
. By (a variant for
(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}
of)
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.4]
,
\CT_{B}(\mathcal{F})\in D^{\ULA}_{\et}(\Gr_{T,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd}
is ULA over
S
. Now
\iota\colon\Gr_{T_{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{T,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
is an open embedding, hence
\ell
-cohomologically smooth, so
\iota^{*}\CT_{B}(\mathcal{F})
is ULA over
S
by
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.13(i)]
.

∎

Definition 5.2.6

(Small Smith operation)
.

The
(small) Smith operation
is the functor

(5.5)

\Psm:=\mathbb{T}^{*}\circ\iota^{*}\colon(D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd})^{B\Sigma}\rightarrow\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd},

which is well-defined by Lemma
5.2.5
.

The functor
\Psm
has an equivariant version, which we also denote

\Psm\colon(D_{\et}^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{G},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\Lambda)^{\bd})^{B\Sigma}\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}(\mathcal{H}\mathrm{ck}_{{H},S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})^{\bd}.

5.2.3.
Compatibilities

We now establish some compatibility statements that could be remembered under the slogan, “the Smith operation commutes with all functors”.

Notation 5.2.7
.

Below we let
Y,Y^{\prime}
be spaces of the form
\Gr_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
, or the
m
-step convolution version thereof, or the twisted Grassmannian
\Gr^{\twi}_{G,S/(\Div^{1}_{X})^{I}}
or (closures of) strata thereof, and
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a map induced by a group homomorphism
G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
, or a convolution map. In all such cases, the small Tate categories of
Y^{\sigma}
and
(Y^{\prime})^{\sigma}
are defined, because of Proposition
4.1.2
and Corollary
4.1.4
. The Smith operation is also defined, and will be denoted

\Psm\colon D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})

where
?
refers to the constructible or equivariant conditions.

Example 5.2.8
.

We allow
G^{\prime}
to be the trivial group, in which case
\Gr_{G^{\prime},S/(\Div_{X}^{1})^{I}}\cong S
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a
\Sigma
-equivariant morphism of the type in Notation
5.2.7
. Let
f^{\sigma}\colon Y^{\sigma}\rightarrow(Y^{\prime})^{\sigma}
be the induced map of fixed points. Since pullbacks preserve stalks, from Lemma
5.2.2
we see that the pullback functor
(f^{\sigma})^{*}\colon D^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}\rightarrow D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}
preserves the perfect subcategories, hence descends to a functor

(5.6)

(f^{\sigma})^{*}\colon\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}).

We have the properties analogous to §
3.4
in this situation. Applying relative Verdier duality to (
5.6
), we also get

(5.7)

(f^{\sigma})^{!}\colon\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}).

Below, when we say that diagrams “canonically commute”, we mean that we construct explicit natural isomorphisms.

Lemma 5.2.9
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a
\Sigma
-equivariant morphism of the type in Notation
5.2.7
. The following diagrams canonically commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\sigma})^{*}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\sigma})^{!}}

Proof.

For the first square, the assertion is immediate from the definitions (the point being that
*
-restrictions commute with
*
-restrictions). The assertion for the second square follows from the first square plus Lemma
3.4.3
, which allows us to replace
\iota^{*}
with
\iota^{!}
in the definition of
\Psm
.
∎

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a
\sigma
-equivariant morphism of the type in Notation
5.2.7
. For
f^{\sigma}\colon Y^{\sigma}\rightarrow(Y^{\prime})^{\sigma}
the induced map of fixed points, Lemma
3.4.5
implies that
\mathrm{R}f_{!}^{\sigma}
and
\mathrm{R}f_{*}^{\sigma}
preserve the perfect subcategories, hence descend to

\mathrm{R}f_{!}^{\sigma}\colon\Perf_{?}^{\ULA}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})

\mathrm{R}f_{*}^{\sigma}\colon\Perf_{?}^{\ULA}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})

Proposition 5.2.10
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a
\Sigma
-equivariant morphism of the type in Notation
5.2.7
. Then the following diagrams canonically commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{!}^{\sigma}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}((Y^{\prime})^{\sigma};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psm}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{*}^{\sigma}}

Proof.

We give the argument for the first diagram, the second being similar
7
7

7

Using also that
Y^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow Y
is
\omega_{0}
-locally extra small (cf. Example
3.1.4
).
. To prove the assertion, we may (thanks to Lemma
5.2.9
) base change to the
\Sigma
-fixed locus of
Y^{\prime}
, and therefore reduce to the case that
\Sigma
acts trivially on
Y^{\prime}
. Let
i\colon Y^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow Y
be the inclusion of the
\Sigma
-fixed points and
j\colon U\hookrightarrow Y
be the complementary open embedding. For
\mathcal{F}\in D^{\ULA}_{?}(Y;\Lambda)^{B\Sigma}
consider the exact triangle

j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}.

Since
\Sigma
acts freely on
U
, Corollary
3.4.6
(1) implies that
\mathrm{R}f_{!}j_{!}j^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda[\Sigma])
, hence projects to
0
in
\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})
. Therefore the map
\mathrm{R}f_{!}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{!}i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}
projects to an isomorphism in
\Perf^{\ULA}_{?}(Y^{\prime};\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda})
.
∎

5.3.
Relative Tate-parity sheaves

We now develop an analogous theory to §
5.1
in the setting of the small Tate category, inspired by work of Leslie-Lonergan
[
LL21
]
which does this for the classical affine Grassmannian. We note that it is important here to take integral coefficients in order to have any hope of parity vanishing properties, because of Example
3.7.1
.

5.3.1.
Preliminary lemmas

Let
\mathbb{O}:=W(k)
, so
k=\mathbb{O}/\ell
. Note that we take
|I|=1
below. Recall (cf. §
2.7
) that we write

\mathbb{F}\colon D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}\rightarrow D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})^{\bd}

for the change-of-coefficients functor.

The following Lemma is parallel to
[
LL21
, Proposition 4.6.1]
.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathbb{O})^{\bd}
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

(5.8)

\Hom_{\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}}(\mathbb{T}\mathcal{F},\mathbb{T}\mathcal{G})\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\Hom_{D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})^{\bd}}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F},\mathbb{F}\mathcal{G}[2i]).

Proof.

We have

\mathcal{H}om_{\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}}(\mathbb{T}\mathcal{F},\mathbb{T}\mathcal{G})\cong\mathbb{T}\left(\mathcal{H}om_{D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\right).

As in Example
3.7.4
, for any
\mathcal{E}\in D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
, we have

\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}\cong\mathrm{Tot}\left(\ldots\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\ell}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{0}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\ell}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{0}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\ldots\right)\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}.

Noting that

\mathrm{Tot}\left(\ldots\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\ell}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{0}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\ell}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{0}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\ldots\right)\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}[2i],

the result then follows upon taking global sections.
∎

Remark 5.3.2
.

The proof of Lemma
5.3.1
did not use the ULA or
L^{+}H
-constructibility hypotheses.

Lemma 5.3.3
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected. Then any object
\mathcal{F}\in\Perf^{\ULA}_{(L^{+}H)}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1},\mu};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})
is a finite direct sum of objects of the form
\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{L})
and
\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{L}[1])
for
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
.

Proof.

The same argument as for
[
LL21
, Theorem 5.4.1]
shows that any such
\mathcal{F}
is pulled back from the small Tate category of
S
. This category is generated by locally constant sheaves, since ULA complexes relative to the identity map are locally constant
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.9]
, and
S
has no higher cohomology. Then we may conclude by applying the argument of
[
LL21
, §3.5.1]
, with the following remark: In
[
LL21
]
the case
\ell=2
is excluded because of
[
LL21
, Remark 3.5.4]
which claims that
\Ext^{1}_{\mathbf{F}_{2}}(\mathbf{F}_{2},\mathbf{F}_{2})\neq 0
, however this is clearly false, so we may also take
\ell=2
.
∎

Lemma 5.3.4
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Then the category
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is Krull-Schmidt.

Proof.

By
[
CYZ08
, Theorem A.1]
, a Karoubian category such that the endomorphism ring of any object is semiperfect is Krull-Schmidt. We will check the semiperfect and Karoubian conditions.

First we check that
\End(\mathcal{K})
is semiperfect for any
\mathcal{K}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
, by showing that
\End(\mathcal{K})
is a finite
k
-algebra. Indeed, it follows from Lemma
5.3.3
and Lemma
5.3.1
that for any
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
, the whole Ext-algebra
\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2})
is finite-dimensional over
k
.

Now it suffices to show that
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is Karoubian, i.e., all idempotents are split. According to
[
LC07
, Proposition 2.3]
, in a triangulated category an idempotent of a distinguished triangle which splits on any two terms splits on the third. Therefore by devissage, we may reduce to showing that for any indecomposable object in
\Perf^{\ULA}_{(L^{+}H)}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1},\mu};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})
, its endomorphism ring is local. By Lemma
5.3.3
such
\mathcal{K}
must be isomorphic to
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{O}^{r}[d])
for some
d
, and then Lemma
5.3.1
computes its endomorphism algebra, which is seen to be local by inspection.

∎

5.3.2.
Relative Tate-parity complexes

We define make definitions within the small Tate category analogous to those in §
5.1.3
.

Definition 5.3.5
.

Let
\mathcal{K}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
. Fix a pariversity
\dagger\colon X_{*}(T_{H})\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
. Below we view Tate cohomology as being indexed by
\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
.

(1)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say
\mathcal{K}
is
relative
?
-Tate-even
if for all geometric points
\overline{s}\rightarrow S
and all
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})^{+}
,

\mathrm{T}^{\dagger(\mu)+1}(i_{\mu}^{?}(\mathcal{K}|_{\overline{s}}))=0.

(2)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say
\mathcal{K}
is
relative
?
-Tate-odd
if
\mathcal{K}[1]
is relative ?-Tate-even.

(3)

For
?\in\{*,!\}
, we say that
\mathcal{K}
is
relative
?
-Tate-parity
if
\mathcal{K}
is either relative
?
-even or relative
?
-odd.

(4)

We say
\mathcal{K}
is
relative Tate-even
(resp.
relative Tate-odd
) if
\mathcal{K}
is both relative
*
-Tate even (resp. odd) and relative
!
-Tate even (resp. odd).

(5)

We say
\mathcal{K}
is a
relative Tate-parity complex
if it is isomorphic within
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
to the direct sum of a relative Tate-even complex and a relative Tate-odd complex. The full subcategory of
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
spanned by relative Tate-parity complexes (with coefficients in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}
) is denoted
\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}})
.

5.3.3.
Modular reduction

We now establish some properties of relative parity complexes under change-of-coefficients, parallel to
[
JMW14
, §2.5]
and
[
LL21
, §5.6]
.

Lemma 5.3.6
.

Recall that
\mathbb{F}
is the change-of-coefficients functor

\mathbb{F}=k\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{O}}(-)\colon D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}\rightarrow D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})^{\bd}.

Let
\mathcal{E}\in D_{(L^{+}{G})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{G},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
. Then:

(1)

\mathcal{E}
is relative ?-even (resp. odd) if and only if
\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}
is relative ?-even (resp. odd).

(2)

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected and
\ell>b(\check{G})
. Then for all
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
, we have

\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathbb{F}L).

Proof.

(1) It is immediate from the definitions that
i^{?}\mathbb{F}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathbb{F}(i^{?}\mathcal{E})
, so
\mathcal{E}
is ?-even (resp. odd) implies that
\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}
is ?-even (resp. odd). The converse follows from the assumption that
\mathcal{E}
has
\mathbb{O}
-free stalks and costalks, so the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{E}
are supported in the same degrees as the cohomology sheaves of
\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}
.

(2) follows from the definitions, using that change-of-coefficients sends tilting modules to tilting modules.
∎

We next explain that the functor
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{T}^{*}\epsilon^{*}
from Definition
5.2.4
has similar properties to modular reduction.

Proposition 5.3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\in D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
.

(1) If
\mathcal{E}\in D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative even (resp. odd), then
\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative Tate-even (resp. odd).

(2) Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Then for all
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})_{+}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
, the object
\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
is relative Tate-parity and indecomposable.

Proof.

As explained in §
5.2.3
, the operation
\mathbb{T}
is compatible with formation of
i_{\mu}^{*}
or
i_{\mu}^{!}
. Hence to prove (1) we reduce to showing that
\mathrm{T}^{i}\epsilon^{*}\mathbb{O}
vanishes in odd degree, which was seen in Example
3.7.1
.

Having established part (1), and using that
\mathcal{E}(\mu,L)
is relative parity, to prove part (2) it only remains to check that
\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}(\mu,L)
is indecomposable. Abbreviate
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}(\mu,L)
. By Lemma
5.3.4
, this is equivalent to the endomorphism ring of
\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}
being local. Applying Lemma
5.3.1
to
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{E}
, we have

(5.9)

\Hom_{\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}}(\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E},\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E})=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\Hom_{D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};k)^{\bd}}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E},\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E}[2i]).

By Lemma
5.3.6
(2), the ring
\Hom_{D_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};k)^{\bd}}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E},\mathbb{F}\mathcal{E})
is local. This shows that the subalgebra on the RHS of (
5.9
) indexed by
i=0
is local, and by perversity the summands of (
5.9
) indexed by
i<0
vanish. This implies the desired locality of the graded algebra (
5.9
).
∎

Definition 5.3.8
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected and
\ell>b(\check{H})
. For
\mu\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
, we define

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}}(\mu,\mathcal{L}):=\mathbb{T}\mathcal{E}(\mu,\mathcal{L})\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}}).

5.3.4.
Structure theory for strictly totally disconnected
S

Here we record several structural results about
\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}

under the assumption
S
is strictly totally disconnected.

Below, all Hom and Ext groups are formed in the category

\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
; we omit this for ease of notation. By arguments analogous to those in §
5.1.4
, we have the following results.

Lemma 5.3.9
.

Assume that
S
is strictly totally disconnected. If
\mathcal{F}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative
*
-Tate-parity and
\mathcal{G}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative
!
-Tate-parity, then we have a (non-canonical) isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

\Ext^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\cong\bigoplus_{\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})^{+}}\Ext^{\bullet}(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{!}\mathcal{G})

Lemma 5.3.10
.

Assume that
S
is strictly totally disconnected. If
\mathcal{F}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative
*
-Tate-even and
\mathcal{G}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
is relative
!
-Tate-odd, then
\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=0
.

Lemma 5.3.11
.

Assume that
S
is strictly totally disconnected. If
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
are either both relative Tate-even or both relative Tate-odd, and
i_{\mu}
is the inclusion of a stratum which is open in the support of both
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, then the restriction map
\Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\Hom(i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F},i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G})
is surjective.

Lemma 5.3.12
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Perf_{(L^{+}{H})}^{\ULA}(\Gr_{{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}})^{\bd}
be an indecomposable Tate-parity complex, and let
j\colon U\rightarrow\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}}
be an inclusion of a union of strata open in the support of
\mathcal{F}
. Then
j^{*}\mathcal{F}
is either
0
or indecomposable.

Proposition 5.3.13
.

Assume
S=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}})
be an indecomposable relative Tate-parity complex. Then
\mathcal{F}
enjoys the following properties:

(1)

\mathcal{F}
has support of the form
\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
for some
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})^{+}
.

(2)

The restriction
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}
to the open stratum in
\supp(\mathcal{F})
is isomorphic
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{O}[d])
for some
d
.

(3)

Any indecomposable relative Tate-parity complex
\mathcal{G}
supported on
\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\leq\mu}
, such that
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{G}\cong\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{O}[d])
, is isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 5.3.14
.

Assume
S
is strictly totally disconnected and
\ell>b(\check{H})
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}})
and
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})^{+}
be maximal so that the support of
\mathcal{F}
intersects
\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
non-trivially. If
i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathbb{T}\mathcal{L}[\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle]
on
\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X},\mu}
for some
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
, then
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
is a retract of
\mathcal{F}
.

(2)

Any
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{O}}})
is a finite direct sum of shifts of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}}(\mu,\mathcal{L})
for various
\mu\in X_{*}(T_{H})^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Loc(S;\mathbb{O})
.

5.4.
Even maps

We define a
stratified v-stack
to be a v-stack
Y
plus a decomposition of
Y
into locally closed strata
i_{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}\hookrightarrow Y
, such that
i_{\mu}
is shriekable for all
\mu
. For a stratified small v-stack
Y
and a pariversity
\dagger
(i.e., a function from the set of strata to
\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
), we may define (absolute)
parity complexes
analogously to Definition
5.1.8
(requiring the restriction to strata to have cohomology sheaves which are
\Lambda
-free local systems). We do not expect it to have good properties in general, but in this section we axiomatize the property that this notion of parity complex will be preserved under “even” maps, which is true in general.

We begin with a simple observation that provides a partial substitute for “Gysin isomorphisms” in
p
-adic geometry (which are not defined, due to the lack of a notion of local complete intersection).

Lemma 5.4.1
.

Let
i\colon Z\rightarrow Y
be a shriekable map of stratified Artin v-stacks which are
\ell
-cohomologically smooth of integral
\ell
-dimension (cf.
[
FS21
, Definition IV.1.17]
). Then
i^{!}
is isomorphic to an even shift of
i^{*}
on étale local systems on
Y
.

Proof.

By base changing to an étale cover of
Y
, we reduce to showing that
i^{!}\Lambda
is isomorphic to an even shift of
\Lambda
. Writing
\mathbb{D}_{(-)}
for the dualizing sheaf and
d,d^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Z}
for the
\ell
-dimensions of
Y,Z
respectively, we have

i^{!}\Lambda\cong i^{!}\mathbb{D}_{Y}[-2d]\cong\mathbb{D}_{Z}[-2d]\cong\Lambda[2d^{\prime}-2d].

∎

The following Definition is in imitation of
[
JMW14
, Definition 2.32, Definition 2.33]
.

Definition 5.4.2

(Even maps)
.

Let
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
be stratified small v-stacks and
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be shriekable. We say that
f
is
stratified
if

(1)

The pre-image of any stratum
Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}
is a union of strata of
Y
.

(2)

For any stratum
Y_{\mu}\subset Y
lying over any stratum
Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}\subset Y^{\prime}
, the restricted map of strata
f_{\lambda}^{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}
is
\ell
-cohomologically smooth with integral
\ell
-dimension.

We further say that
f
is
even
if for all
\mu,\lambda
and any
\Lambda
-free étale local system
\mathcal{L}
on
Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}
, the complex
\mathrm{R}^{i}(f_{\lambda}^{\mu})_{*}(\mathcal{L})
is
\Lambda
-free and vanishes for odd
i
.

The significance of even maps lies in the fact that their direct image preserves parity, as in the following Proposition.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

Let
Y,Y^{\prime}
be stratified Artin v-stacks and let
f\colon Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
be an even stratified map (hence shriekable by definition). Suppose that all strata of
Y,Y^{\prime}
are
\ell
-cohomologically smooth with integral
\ell
-dimension. Let
\dagger
be a pariversity on
Y^{\prime}
, and define the pullback pariversity
\dagger_{Y}
on
Y
via
\dagger_{Y}(\mu):=\dagger(\lambda)
if
f
carries
Y_{\mu}
to
Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}
.

(1) If
f
is proper and
\mathcal{K}\in D_{\et}^{b}(Y;\Lambda)
is a
\dagger_{Y}
-parity complex, then
\mathrm{R}f_{*}(\mathcal{K})\in D_{\et}^{b}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)
is a
\dagger
-parity complex.

(2) If
f
is smooth of integral
\ell
-dimension and
\mathcal{K}\in D_{\et}^{b}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)
is a
\dagger
-parity complex, then
f^{*}\mathcal{K}\in D_{\et}^{b}(Y;\Lambda)
is a
\dagger_{Y}
-parity complex.

Proof.

(1) The statement and argument are the same as for
[
JMW14
, Proposition 2.34]
, so we just sketch it. Using proper base change, we calculate
i_{\lambda}^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{!}\mathcal{K}
and
i_{\lambda}^{!}\mathrm{R}f_{*}\mathcal{K}
by stratifying the fibers by
Y_{\mu}
, and calculating the cohomology of fibers in terms of cohomology of strata using excision sequences. This equips
i_{\lambda}^{*}\mathrm{R}f_{!}\mathcal{K}
with a filtration whose associated graded is a direct sum of pieces of the form
\mathrm{R}(f_{\lambda}^{\mu})_{!}i_{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{K}
, so its cohomology sheaves are
\Lambda
-free and vanish in the correct parity of degrees by the definition of an even map. The story is similar for
i_{\lambda}^{!}\mathrm{R}f_{*}\mathcal{K}
.

(2) It is immediate from the definitions that
f^{*}\mathcal{K}
is
*
-parity with respect to
\dagger_{Y}
. Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}(Y_{\lambda}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\mu}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\lambda}}

where the right square is Cartesian. Suppose
\mathcal{K}\in D_{\et}^{b}(Y^{\prime};\Lambda)
is
\dagger
-parity. Since
f
is smooth of integral
\ell
-dimension,
(i_{\lambda}^{\prime})^{!}f^{*}\mathcal{K}\cong f_{\lambda}^{*}i_{\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{K}
has locally constant
\Lambda
-free cohomology sheaves, which can only be non-zero in degrees congruent to
\dagger(\lambda)
mod 2. By Lemma
5.4.1
, the same holds for
(i^{\mu}_{\lambda})^{!}(i_{\lambda}^{\prime})^{!}f^{*}\mathcal{K}\cong i_{\mu}^{!}f^{*}\mathcal{K}
, so
f^{*}\mathcal{K}
is also
!
-parity with respect to
\dagger_{Y}
.
∎

5.5.
Demazure resolutions

Let
S
be a small v-stack and
S\rightarrow\Div^{1}_{X}
a map factoring over
\Spd C
where
C=\widehat{\overline{E}}
. We recall the notions of parahoric group schemes and their partial affine flag varieties in the context of
\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-affine Grassmannians, following
[
FS21
, §VI.5]
. Let
A:=W_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathcal{O}_{C^{\flat}})
. For a subset
J
of affine simple roots there is a parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{P}_{J}\rightarrow G_{A}
, and we define the group diamond
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J}/\Spd\mathcal{O}_{C}
by
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J}(R,R^{+})=\mathcal{P}_{J}(\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(R^{\sharp}))
.

Now we define Bott-Samelson varieties in a parallel manner to
[
JMW14
, §4.1]
. Fix a chain

of subsets of the affine simple roots of
G
,

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right).

For
(1\leq i\leq k\leq n)
, we define

\Gr_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}=LG\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{i}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{i+1}})\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{i+1}}}\times\ldots\times(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{k-1}})\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{k-1}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{k}})/(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{k}})

and the
Bott-Samelson variety

\BS_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}=(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{i}})\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{i}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{i+1}})\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{i+1}}}\times\ldots\times(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{k-1}})\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{k-1}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{k}})/(L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I_{k}})

where the action maps are given by the same formulas as in
[
JMW14
, p.1201]
. There is an obvious closed embedding
\BS_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}
induced by the closed embedding
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J_{i}}\hookrightarrow LG
.

Example 5.5.1
.

If
I_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i
, and
J_{i}=\{j_{i}\}
is a singleton with corresponding simple reflection
s_{j_{i}}
, then
\BS_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}=\mathrm{Dem}_{\dot{w},S}
is the
Demazure variety
from
[
FS21
, Definition VI.5.6]
for
\dot{w}=s_{j_{i}}s_{j_{2}}\ldots s_{j_{k}}\in W_{\mathrm{aff}}
(the affine Weyl group of
G
).

Consider
LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I}
with the stratification by left
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I^{\prime}}
-orbits for some
\mathcal{P}_{I^{\prime}}
. Note that if
I\subset J
, then there is a natural map

\pi\colon LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I}\rightarrow LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J}

which is stratified and even (in particular, shriekable), as well as proper. For any
I
, a maximal proper choice of
J
gives a map from
LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I}
to an affine Grassmannian
\Gr_{G,C}
, and we use it to pull back to the dimension pariversity to
LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I}
. Note that
\Gr_{J_{\bullet}}^{(i,\ldots,k)}
maps to
\Gr_{G,C}
via projection to its first factor; this map is again stratified and even, and we use it to pull back the pariversity
\dagger
. Below, “parity” will always refer to this pariversity.

Lemma 5.5.2
.

If
I\subset J
, and
I^{\prime}\subset J^{\prime}
, the projection map

\pi\colon LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I}\rightarrow LG/L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J}

is an even stratified map (where the source is equipped with the stratification by left
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{I^{\prime}}
-orbits, and the target equipped with the stratification by left
L^{+}\mathcal{P}_{J^{\prime}}
-orbits), which is both proper and
\ell
-cohomologically smooth of integral
\ell
-dimension.

Proof.

The map
\pi
is a torsor for the diamond of a partial flag variety of
G
, hence it is proper and
\ell
-cohomologically smooth (of integral
\ell
