We record some general properties of the Geometric Satake equivalence. If
G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
is a central isogeny, then the induced map
f\colon\Gr_{G,\Div_{X}^{1}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G^{\prime},\Div_{X}^{1}}
restricts to isomorphisms of connected components, and the restriction map
\Rep_{k}({}^{L}G^{\prime})\rightarrow\Rep_{k}({}^{L}G)
is intertwined under Geometric Satake with the (derived) pushforward functor

(10.5)

\Sat(\Gr_{G,\Div_{X}^{1}};k)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{R}f_{*}}\Sat(\Gr_{G^{\prime},\Div_{X}^{1}};k).

We record the following general property of the Brauer functor.

Lemma 10.2.3
.

Assume
\ell>\max\{b(\check{G}),b(\check{H})\}
. Write
\overline{H}:=H/(H\cap Z(G))
and assume that
\overline{H}=(G_{\ad})^{\sigma}
. Then the diagram

(10.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat(\Gr_{\overline{H},\Div_{X}^{1}};k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat(\Gr_{H,\Div_{X}^{1}};k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat(\Gr_{G_{\ad},\Div_{X}^{1}};k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sat(\Gr_{G,\Div_{X}^{1}};k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{br}_{\ad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{br}}

commutes, where the horizontal maps are of the form (
10.5
).

Proof.

Write
f\colon\Gr_{H,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\overline{H},S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
and
g\colon\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G_{\ad},S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
for the projections induced by the quotients by
Z(G)
. By construction of
\widetilde{br}
and
\widetilde{br}_{\ad}
, it suffices to show that the diagram

(10.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{\overline{H},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{H,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G_{\ad},S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces br_{\ad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces br}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}g_{*}}

commutes for any strictly totally disconnected
S
over
\Div_{X}^{1}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{\mathbb{O}})
and
\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{O}}k\in\Parity^{\ULA}(\Gr_{G,S/\Div^{1}_{X}};{k})
. Then we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}f_{*}\circ br(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{R}f_{*}\circ L\circ[\dagger_{H}^{G}]\circ\Psm\circ\Nm^{(\ell^{-1})}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\cong}}L\circ\mathrm{R}f_{*}\circ[\dagger_{H}^{G}]\circ\Psm\circ\Nm^{(\ell^{-1})}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\cong}}L\circ[\dagger_{\overline{H}}^{G_{\ad}}]\circ\mathrm{R}f_{*}\circ\Psm\circ\Nm^{(\ell^{-1})}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\cong}}L\circ[\dagger_{\overline{H}}^{G_{\ad}}]\circ\Psm\circ\mathrm{R}g_{*}\circ\Nm^{(\ell^{-1})}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{\cong}}L\circ[\dagger_{\overline{H}}^{G_{\ad}}]\circ\Psm\circ\Nm^{(\ell^{-1})}\circ\mathrm{R}g_{*}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=br_{\ad}\circ\mathrm{R}g_{*}(\mathbb{F}\mathcal{F})

whose composition gives the desired commutativity. Here, the natural isomorphisms are justified as follows:

(1)

is evident from the fact that
\mathrm{R}f_{*}
preserves normalized parity sheaves, because
f
restricts to an isomorphism between connected components of the source and target.

(2)

holds because
G
and
G_{\ad}
differ by a central quotient, and similarly for
H
and
\overline{H}
, so
[\dagger_{H}^{G}]=[\dagger_{\overline{H}}^{G_{\ad}}]
; and clearly shifts commute with
\mathrm{R}f_{*}
.

(3)

holds by Proposition
5.2.10
, using Proposition
4.1.2
to see that applying
\Sigma
-fixed points to the map
g\colon\Gr_{G,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G_{\ad},S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
yields
f\colon\Gr_{H,S/\Div_{X}^{1}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\overline{H},S/\Div_{X}^{1}}
.

(4)

holds because
\mathrm{R}g_{*}
is symmetric monoidal.

∎

Proof of Proposition
10.2.1
.

Combining Lemma
10.2.3
with Tannakian reconstruction, we find that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}G_{\ad}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\psi_{\ad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\psi}

commutes. As mentioned above, Corollary
7.6.7
implies that it factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}G_{\ad}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\psi_{\ad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fr_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fr_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{\ad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}

By inspection the bottom square induces isomorphism on the cokernels of the rows, hence is a pushout square. As explained in §
10.1.3
, Lemma
10.2.2
implies that
\zeta_{\ad}
is
\widehat{G}
-conjugate to the canonical
{}^{L}j_{\ad}
. Since
\zeta
is pushed out from
\zeta_{\ad}
and
{}^{L}j
is pushed out from
{}^{L}j_{\ad}
, we deduce that
\zeta
is
\widehat{G}
-conjugate to
{}^{L}j
, and then that
{}^{L}\psi
is
\check{G}
-conjugate to
{}^{L}j\circ\Fr_{\ell}
, as desired.
∎

10.3.
Tate cohomology of deep level Deligne-Lusztig varieties

We briefly recall the generalized Deligne-Lusztig representations appearing in
[
CI21
]
.

10.3.1.
Group-theoretic setup

Let
T\hookrightarrow G
be an unramified maximal torus and
x\in\mathcal{B}(G/E)
be a point of the Bruhat-Tits building of
G
that lies in the apartment of
T
. Corresponding to
x
we have by Bruhat-Tits theory a parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{O}_{E}
, whose generic fiber is
G/E
.

Recall that
\mathbf{F}_{q}
is the residue field of
E
. By assumption,
T
splits over
\breve{E}
. Choose a
\breve{E}
-rational Borel subgroup of
G_{\breve{E}}
containing
T_{\breve{E}}
, and let
U
be its unipotent radical.

For
r\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have group schemes
\mathbb{G}_{r},\mathbb{T}_{r}
over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
as in
[
CO
, §6.1]
8
8

8

The indexing seems to differ from that of
[
CI21
, §2.5, 2.6]
by
1
.
corresponding to subquotients of the Moy-Prasad filtration at
x
, such that

\mathbb{G}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})=G_{x,0:r+}:=G_{x,0}/G_{x,r+}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})=T_{0:r+}:=T_{x,0}/T_{x,r+}.

We also have a group scheme
\mathbb{U}_{r}\subset(\mathbb{G}_{r})_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}
corresponding to
U
.

10.3.2.
Deep level Deligne-Lusztig varieties

Let
\Fr_{q}
be the
q
-power Frobenius for schemes over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
. We recall certain schemes constructed in
[
CI21
, §4]
: the “deep level Deligne-Lusztig varieties”

S_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}:=\{x\in(\mathbb{G}_{r})_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}\colon x^{-1}\Fr_{q}(x)\in\mathbb{U}_{r}\}.

(The variety
S_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}
is called
X_{r}
in
[
CO
]
.) It is a separated, smooth, finite type scheme over
\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}
, with an action of
\mathbb{G}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})\times\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
by multiplication on the left and right.The action of
\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
is free, and we define

X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}:=S_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}/\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q}).

Example 10.3.1
.

When
r=0
, the definition of
X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}
specializes to that of a classical Deligne-Lusztig variety from
[
DL76
]
.

Definition 10.3.2

(Deep level Deligne-Lusztig induction)
.

Let
\theta\colon\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})\rightarrow k^{\times}
be a character. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
be the corresponding local system on
X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}
. We define

R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta):=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}^{*}(X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}};\mathcal{L}_{\theta})\in D^{b}(\Rep_{k}\mathbb{G}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})).

10.3.3.
Calculation of Tate cohomology

Following
[
CI21
, §2.8]
, we define
W_{x}(T)
to be the subgroup of
W(T,G)
generated by vector parts of affine roots
\psi
of
G
for which
\psi(x)=0
.

Following
[
CI21
, Definition 5.1]
, we say that
s\in G_{x}(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}})
is
unramified very regular
(with respect to
x
) if
s
is regular semisimple in
G_{\breve{E}}
, its connected centralizer
Z^{\circ}_{G}(s)
is an
\breve{E}
-split maximal torus of
G_{\breve{E}}
, and
\alpha(s)\not\equiv 1\pmod{\varpi_{E}}
for all roots
\alpha
of
Z^{\circ}_{G}(s)
. We say that
s\in\mathbb{G}_{r}(\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q})
is unramified very regular if it is the image of an unramified very regular element of
G_{x}(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}})
.

Proposition 10.3.3
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
be unramified very regular in
\mathbb{G}_{r}(\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q})
and of order
\ell
, and let
\sigma=\mathrm{conj}_{s}
as an automorphism of
G
. Then for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
we have

(10.8)

\mathrm{T}^{j}(R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta))\cong\bigoplus_{{w\in W_{x}(T)^{\Fr_{q}}}}\theta^{w}\in D^{b}(\Rep_{k}\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})).

where
\theta^{w}=\theta\circ w^{-1}
is the translate of
\theta
by the action of
w
.

Proof.

By
[
CI21
, Proposition 5.6]
, we have

(10.9)

\Fix(\sigma,X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}})=\bigcup_{w\in W_{x}(T)^{\Fr_{q}}}[\dot{w}]

where
[\dot{w}]
is the coset
\dot{w}\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
of any lift
\dot{w}\in\mathbb{G}_{r}(\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q})
of
w
.

By
[
Fen24
, §A.1.2]
(which is the scheme-theoretic counterpart to §
5.2.3
), this implies that

\mathrm{T}^{j}(R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta))=\mathrm{T}^{j}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}^{*}(X_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}};\mathcal{L}_{\theta}))\cong\mathrm{T}^{j}(\bigcup_{w\in W_{x}(T)^{\Fr_{q}}}[\dot{w}];\mathcal{L}_{\theta})\cong\bigoplus_{w\in W_{x}(T)^{\Fr_{q}}}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}|_{[\dot{w}]}.

Here
\mathcal{L}|_{[\dot{w}]}
is a
\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
-equivariant sheaf on a point, i.e., a representation of
\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
. Writing
t\dot{w}=\dot{w}(\dot{w}^{-1}t\dot{w})
, we see that
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}|_{[\dot{w}]}\cong\theta^{w}\in\Rep_{k}\mathbb{T}_{r}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
.

∎

10.4.
Toral compact inductions

We may regard
R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta)
as a (derived) smooth representation of
G_{x,0}
by inflation.
Choose some extension of
\theta
to
T(E)
, which we use to regard
R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta)
as a (derived) smooth representation of
T(E)G_{x,0}
. Then we define

\pi_{T,U,\theta}:=\cInd_{T(E)G_{x,0}}^{G(E)}R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta)\in D^{b}(\Rep^{\sm}_{k}G(E)).

Note that since
\sigma
is inner, Example
9.1.3
applies to equip any
\Pi\in D^{b}(\Rep^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}G(E))
with a canonical
\Sigma
-equivariant structure. In particular, we use this to view
\pi_{T,U,\theta}\in D^{b}(\Rep^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}G(E))^{B\Sigma}
.

In preparation for calculating the Tate cohomology of
\pi_{T,U,\theta}
, we study the interaction between compact induction and Tate cohomology. Let
K\subset G(E)
be any
\Sigma
-stable closed subgroup. Let
Y:=G(E)/K
. Then there is a functor from finite-dimensional representations of
K
to
G(E)
-equivariant local systems on
Y
, which we denote
V\mapsto\mathcal{F}(V)
. In turn, there is a functor from
G(E)
-equivariant local systems on
Y
to smooth
G(E)
-representations, obtained by taking compactly supported global sections. Assuming that
K
is open, the composite functor

V\mapsto\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y;\mathcal{F}(V))

is the compact induction from
K
to
G(E)
. By
[
TV16
, §3.3]
we have, for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
, a natural isomorphism

(10.10)

\mathrm{T}^{j}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y;\mathcal{F}))\cong\mathrm{T}^{j}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y^{\sigma};\mathcal{F}))\in\Rep_{k}^{\mathrm{sm}}H(E).

for any
G(E)
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{F}
on
Y
.

Theorem 10.4.1
.

Let
T<G
be an elliptic unramified maximal torus. Assume that
T(E)
contains an element
s
of order
\ell
, which is unramified very regular with respect to
x\in\mathcal{B}(G/E)
and strongly regular in
G_{\ad}(E)
. Then for any
\theta\colon T(E)\rightarrow k^{\times}
,
\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}(\mathrm{H}^{*}(\pi_{T,U,\theta}))
contains the point of
\Spec\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})
corresponding to the semi-simple
L
-parameter

(10.11)

W_{E}\xrightarrow{{}^{L}\theta}{}^{L}T(k)\xrightarrow{{}^{L}j}{}^{L}G(k)

where
{}^{L}\theta
is the
L
-parameter given by class field theory, and
{}^{L}j
is canonical
L
-embedding of
T
(§
10.1.3
).

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Aut(G)
by the conjugation by
s
, so
G^{\sigma}=T
. Note that the hypotheses on
s
imply that Proposition
10.2.1
holds in this situation.

Let
K=T(E)G_{x,0}
. Since
T
is elliptic, we have
K=Z_{G}G_{x,0}
where
Z_{G}
is the center of
G(E)
. Note that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)
is finite, as it has a finite-index subgroup of the form
Z_{G}
times a pro-
p
subgroup and
p\neq\ell=|\Sigma|
. From the long exact sequence in non-abelian cohomology

1\rightarrow K^{\sigma}\rightarrow G(E)^{\sigma}\rightarrow(G(E)/K)^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;G(E))

and the torsor-shifting discussion in
[
Ser94
, §5.3, 5.4]
, we see that

(G(E)/K)^{\sigma}=\bigcup_{\xi\in\ker[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;G(E))]}G(E)^{\sigma}/({}_{\xi}K)^{\sigma}

where
{}_{\xi}K
is the twist of
K
corresponding to the torsor
\xi
. Henceforth we abbreviate

\ker^{1}(\Sigma;K,G):=\ker[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;G(E))].

Since
({}_{\xi}K)^{\sigma}\supset({}_{\xi}T(E())^{\sigma}=T(E)=G(E)^{\sigma}
, we see that each
G(E)^{\sigma}/({}_{\xi}K)^{\sigma}
is a point, which we will denote
[\xi]\in(G(E)/K)^{\sigma}
. From (
10.10
) we get, for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
, an equivariant isomorphism

\mathrm{T}^{j}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})\cong\bigoplus_{\xi\in\ker^{1}(\Sigma;K,G)}\mathrm{T}^{j}(\mathcal{F}(R^{\mathbb{G}_{r}}_{\mathbb{T}_{r},\mathbb{U}_{r}}(\theta))_{[\xi]})\in D^{b}(\Rep^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}T(E)).

In particular, taking
\xi=1\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Sigma;K)
to be the trivial class, we see that from Proposition
10.3.3
that a direct sum of Weyl conjugates of
\theta
appears as a direct summand of
\mathrm{T}^{j}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})
, as a representation of
T(E)
.

By Theorem
9.1.1
, we deduce that
F_{*}\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})
contains the image under
{}^{L}\psi
of
\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{T})}\theta^{w}
for various
w\in W(T,G)
. For tori, the Fargues-Scholze correspondence is known to be compatible with the usual Local Langlands Correspondence, so
\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{T})}\theta^{w}
is the point corresponding to the
L
-parameter of class field theory,

(10.12)

W_{E}\xrightarrow{{}^{L}\theta^{w}}{}^{L}T(k).

Note that
{}^{L}\psi\circ{}^{L}\theta^{w}={}^{L}\psi\circ{}^{L}\theta\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{E};\widehat{G}(k))
for each
w\in W
, since the Weyl conjugation becomes inner in
\widehat{G}
.

By Proposition
10.2.1
we have

{}^{L}\psi\circ{}^{L}\theta={}^{L}j\circ\Fr_{\ell}\circ{}^{L}\theta=\Fr_{\ell}\circ{}^{L}j\circ{}^{L}\theta\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{E};\widehat{G}(k))

so we deduce that
F_{*}\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})
contains
\Fr_{\ell}\circ{}^{L}j\circ{}^{L}\theta
. By inspection of the definition, Frobenius (un)twisting is compatible with the Fargues-Scholze correspondence in the sense that

(10.13)

F_{*}\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}\Pi=\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}F_{*}\Pi=\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}\Pi^{(\ell)}

so
\supp_{\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})
contains the point of
\Exc_{k}(W_{E},\widehat{G})
corresponding to
{}^{L}j\circ{}^{L}\theta\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{E};\widehat{G}(k))
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 10.4.2
.

There is a non-zero irreducible
G(E)
-subquotient
\Pi
of
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\pi_{T,U,\theta})
, for some
i
, such that
\rho_{\Pi}
is (
10.11
). In particular, if
\pi_{T,U,\theta}
is concentrated in a single cohomological degree and is irreducible, then its Fargues-Scholze parameter is (
10.11
).

Remark 10.4.3
.

Note that Theorem
10.4.1
imposes no regularity conditions on
\theta
. We expect that if
\theta
is sufficiently regular, and
\ell
is not too small, then
\pi_{T,U,\theta}
should be concentrated in a single cohomological degree and irreducible, and cuspidal. There are interesting classes of examples where this is known; a notable one pertains to the
depth-zero supercuspidals
studied in
[
KV06
,
DR09
]
, which correspond to the case where:

•

x\in\mathcal{B}(G/E)
is a vertex (so
G_{x}
is a maximal parahoric),

•

r=0
, so
X_{\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{U}_{0}}
is a usual Deligne-Lusztig variety,

•

T
is not contained in a proper parabolic subgroup and
\theta
is non-singular (meaning that it is not orthogonal to any coroot, cf.
[
DL76
, Definition 5.15(1)]
), so its Deligne-Lusztig induction is cuspidal.

Letting
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
be the character sheaf on
X_{\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{U}_{0}}
associated with the non-singular character
\theta\colon T(\mathbf{F}_{q})\rightarrow k^{\times}
, it follows from
[
DL76
, Lemma 9.14]
and
[
Bro90
, Lemma 3.5]
that in this situation
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{c}(X_{\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{U}_{0}};\mathcal{L}_{\theta})
concentrates in a single degree; and if the prime-to-
\ell
component of
\theta
is in general position, then
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{c}(X_{\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{U}_{0}};\mathcal{L}_{\theta})
is irreducible by
[
Bro90
, Lemma 3.6]
.

We expect the
Howe-unramified toral supercuspidal representations
of
[
CO
]
to supply further examples, of arbitrary depth. This is the subject of current work-in-progress.
